# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

Any shape found by looking must be DECLARED BEFORE it is measured, and measured on the TAIL ([[measure-the-tail-not-the-median]]).** ⚠ **And checked against `must_not_miss_eps.py` first — the real-EP list may never shrink.** Verbatim: `operator_shared_notes.md` §"open-ended chart reading". >> 📖 **2026-09-06 — HE SENT A MODEL-BOOK PAGE (AMZN 2020, TraderLion) AND IT READS PERFECTLY. He has DOZENS more and will scan them if useful.** ▶ **SIX THINGS THIS ONE PAGE NAMES THAT WE DO NOT COMPUTE:** **(1) THE 23-EMA IS THE SPINE —** four separate annotations: every add is stopped at it, the trend is *"clear respect for its 23-EMA throughout the length of its move higher"*, and the run ends when price closes below it on volume. **We compute no 23-EMA anywhere; our stop is `entry−2R` off a ONE-MINUTE range while this trails an MA for the life of the move.** **(2) A NAMED BASE VOCABULARY** — pocket pivots (*"5-day PP"*, *"PP volume signature"*), prior resistance becoming support, *"tight, sideways on light volume"* as the constructive state. **This IS the taxonomy he asked for two messages earlier, already written down.** **(3) RSNHBP — relative strength making new highs BEFORE price, and throughout the base.** We rank RS percentiles; we never check whether RS leads price. **(4) BEHAVIOUR IN A CORRECTION AS A QUALITY SCREEN** — −25.61% against a −35.41% market. Cheap to compute, absent from our stack. **(5) VOLUME ABSENCE AS A NEGATIVE** — *"volume was below average as AMZN made new highs, which is a sign of weakness"*. We treat volume as positive-only. **(6) THE EXIT IS A GRADED SEQUENCE, NOT A LEVEL** — extended above the 10-DMA + big-volume negative reversal → partials; closing below 23-EMA/50-DMA → sell some or all; volume expansion on the first close below the 23-EMA since breakout → run over. **Three signals, none an R multiple.** ▶ 📊 **AND THE FUNDAMENTALS STRIP CONTRADICTS OUR RUBRIC: Q1-2020 EPS FELL 29% and AMZN was still a model-book leader** (revenue 21→26→40→37%, EPS −29→+97→+192%). **A single weak EPS quarter did not disqualify it — our axis-2 EPS trajectory would have marked it down.** ⚠ **ONE page, one name, and a winner chosen FOR a model book — survivorship by construction. Source of HYPOTHESES to declare then test, never evidence.** >> 🔬 **2026-09-06 — MULTI-YEAR CLEARANCE TEST RUN ON THE NEW HISTORY. THE PRE-DECLARED MEASURE FAILS, AND IT FAILS INFORMATIVELY.** ▶ **Declared IN THE PROBE BEFORE any number was computed** (`scripts/probes/_594_multiyear_clearance.py`): `clearance_pct` = the % of daily HIGHS in the prior 5 years that the GAP-DAY CLOSE exceeds. One number, no knobs, no alternates tried — the literal reading of *"cleared most of it into top of previous range highs over multiple years"*. ▶ **RESULT: approved median **84.7%** (n=6) vs BAD_CHART median **97.3%** (n=17) — a separation of **−12.6 points**. INVERTED. His bad charts clear MORE overhead supply than the ones he approves.** ▶ 🔑 **WHY, and this is the useful part: six of the seventeen BAD names sit at 99-100% — AVGU, CAR 04-21, QH, AEHR, MRAM, NVTS all gapped to effectively NEW HIGHS, and he called every one of them bad. Meanwhile RNG, the only chart he has ever called GOOD, is at 80.2% — NOT at new highs, exactly as he described it (*"cleared MOST of it"*).** **So a clearance percentile is NOT what he means. A junk stock gapping to an all-time high scores 100% on it. What he is judging is the QUALITY AND SHAPE of the base being cleared, not the fraction cleared.** ▶ ⚠ **POST-HOC OBSERVATION, FLAGGED AS SUCH AND NOT A FINDING — I noticed it in the output, it was NOT declared, and reporting it as evidence would be the exact trap I wrote into this line yesterday: the approved names have **1,224** prior bars at median (minimum 1,146) while the BAD names have **655** (minimum 150), and **7 of 17 bad names have under 400 bars versus 0 of 6 approved**. i.e. many of his "garbage" calls are recent listings with no multi-year base to speak of. THAT IS A HYPOTHESIS FOR A DECLARED TEST, NOT A RESULT.** ▶ **NET: the backfill was still worth it — this test was IMPOSSIBLE two hours ago (RNG had 275 bars, now 1,224) and the 0.496 structure null still deserves a re-run on 5 years. But the first structural measure we could finally compute does not separate his labels.** >> ⚠ **2026-09-06 — THE BACKFILL INTRODUCED A MONEY-PATH LATENCY REGRESSION AND I CAUGHT IT BEFORE PARKING. FIXED.** ▶ **`mi_daily_closes` went 3.3M → 13.6M rows, and its only index was `idx_daily_closes_ticker` — TICKER ONLY. So every per-ticker window query fetched EVERY bar for that ticker and filtered by date afterwards: a single-ticker lookup went to **111 ms** on prod (EXPLAIN ANALYZE, not estimated).** ▶ 🔴 **THAT SHAPE IS ON THE MONEY PATH: `backtester/filters.compute_atr_14` runs it PER CANDIDATE during the 09:31-09:44 ORB window, so ~20 candidates meant roughly 2 SECONDS of avoidable latency sitting in front of order submission** — and ATR feeds `validate_orb_entry`'s stop-width gate. ▶ **FIX: composite `idx_daily_closes_ticker_date ON mi_daily_closes(ticker, trade_date)`, created CONCURRENTLY on prod (no lock) and added to the schema DDL in `db.py` so a rebuild or a disaster-recovery restore recreates it.** ▶ **MEASURED AFTER: the general query 111 ms → **2.09 ms** (53×); the ATR shape → **0.131 ms**.** ▶ **Also checked before parking, and all clean: tree committed, both probe scripts (`_backfill_daily_history.py`, `_594_multiyear_clearance.py`) are IN the repo rather than only in the container, and all 32 `FROM mi_daily_closes` call sites in `db.py` carry a `trade_date` bound — none was left unbounded to scan the new 13.6M rows.** ⚠ **The lesson worth keeping: a data backfill is a performance change. I priced storage and API calls up front and did NOT price the read path, which is where the only real risk was.** ▶ ⏸ **PARKED 2026-09-06 at a clean point, on the operator's check. THE TRIGGER TO RESUME IS HIS SCANS, not a date — he is scanning the rest of the model book (*"i'll work on scanning those… will take some time"*), and those pages are the only input that materially changes what can be learned. 2026-09-20 is a REVIEW date, not a deadline.** ▶ 🔴 **AND THE REAL ANSWER TO *"when should we pick this up"* IS A GAP: this line says the loop should run on *"a recurring `data_gated_reviews.yaml` entry so the cadence is mechanical and not remembered"* — **THAT ENTRY DOES NOT EXIST.** Both chart-related reviews in the YAML (`chart_vision_axis_shadow_decision`, `ep_cooldown_resetup_admission`) are `status=done`. **So today the cadence depends entirely on me remembering, which is precisely what the task says it must not.** Creating that entry is the fix and it is small.** ▶ **What can proceed WITHOUT his scans, both small: re-run the 0.496 structure null on the 5-year history (it was measured on 13 months and is this task's stated bar to beat), and properly DECLARE-then-test the base-length hypothesis I flagged post-hoc today (approved names 1,224 prior bars median vs 655 for bad).** [b1] ▶ ✅ **CADENCE ENTRY BUILT 2026-09-06 — the gap is closed. `chart_reading_review_cycle` is now in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` (153 entries, ids unique), `kind: cadence`, threshold 40 new alerts since 2026-09-06, earliest 2026-09-20.** So the loop no longer depends on me remembering — which is what this line always said it must not, and what it silently did until he asked. ▶ **The entry carries the METHOD, not just a date: build the sample BALANCED BOTH WAYS (HIGH-that-collapsed AND below-bar-that-ran) because his *"our chart vision is spotting the opposite thing"* was only visible BECAUSE both directions were shown; file every ruling to the fixture keyed on (ticker, DATE) with his words verbatim; transcribe anything he shares the SAME turn AND save the image; re-check `test_577_must_not_miss_eps` before anything else. And the trap, in writing: any shape proposed from LOOKING must be declared before it is measured, with the 2026-09-06 clearance test named as the worked example of doing it right — pre-declared, and it failed.** ⚠ **The suite caught my first version: `test_registry_has_no_undeclared_population_gaps` (#573) failed because the entry had no `discriminates_on`. Now declared as INTENTIONALLY unfiltered — all alerts, both account modes, every signal_type — with a note saying why: a timer asking "is there enough new material" must not be filtered to one strategy's cohort, or it hides the cross-strategy disagreements the loop exists to find.** Suite 7649. >> 🎯 **DoD (written 2026-09-10): **closes on an EDGE OR ON A RULED DEAD END, never on a ship** — the operator's own framing. Concretely: enough labelled charts that the read's accuracy is measurable against his labels, and then a ruling. WOULD-FAIL-IF: the loop runs indefinitely with no accuracy number ever put in front of him, which is how a standing program becomes a standing excuse.**

Found while checking whether his "delayed entry with EP day close reclaim" idea already existed — it did, as one of four patterns. ▶ **WHAT IT IS:** every name the EP scan sees joins on its EP day and is watched 20 sessions; `delayed_entry_shadow.py` records raw path facts and fires four patterns, each with its own buy AND stop: **`ep_low_reclaim`** (undercut the EP-day low, 5-min close back above it; stop = lowest low since the undercut) · **`ep_close_reclaim`** (never reaches the EP-day low, dips under the CLOSE, 5-min close back above; stop = the low of the dip) — **this is HIS idea, built 2026-08-30** · **`ep_high_break`** (no pullback, pushes through the EP-day high; stop = prior session low) · **`ep_close_620_prox`** (proximity fallback, 620 turn near the EP-day close). ▶ 🛑 **PASSIVE OBSERVER — THE LINE:** writes only `mi_delayed_entry_watch` / `mi_delayed_entry_trigger`, never trade state; imports nothing from `broker/`; **SILENT — no Telegram while evidence accrues** (his 2026-08-30 ruling: an unproven signal in his notifications becomes a de-facto trade signal). ▶ ⏳ **NOT JUDGEABLE UNTIL ~LATE SEPT, AND ITS CURRENT NUMBERS ARE A TRAP:** oldest fire 2026-08-25, **NINE sessions elapsed against a TWENTY-session window**. A stop settles the instant it is hit, so the 2,334 settled rows are **the losers by construction** and the 696 still-open rows are where any winner lives. That is why every `r_none_*` column reads exactly −1.00 with zero positives (that arm has no target — it can only end at its stop) and the trail arm reads −0.62 to −0.75. **`ep_close_reclaim` currently shows the worst trail average of the four (−0.75R, 8.5% positive, n=623) and quoting that as a verdict on his idea would be a false negative off half a population.** ▶ ⚠ **Exclude `stop_width_pct < 0.5` from every read** — `mfe_r` carries BCTX at 5499R, the #621/#623 near-zero-stop class (99 of 3,129 rows). ▶ **ETA 2026-09-22 = the first date the earliest fires complete 20 sessions and the lane can be read honestly.** DoD: a tail-rate read per pattern on settled 20-session outcomes, measured on the TAIL not the median (`analysis_standard.md` §THE STATISTIC), with the near-zero-stop rows excluded → then his call on whether any pattern graduates. T2.] >> 🔴 **A NAMED CASE CLASS, operator 2026-08-27 — a two-day Fading dip cost the theme bonus at exactly the moment the EP fired, and the EP itself revived the theme.** His words: *"the theme crwd was in was fading [for] a couple days so it didn't get included in [the] rubric, but the EP gave the group a boost and I see now the theme is back to mainstream, no immediate action but we need to see what to do on these cases."* **Verified in `mi_themes`, Network Security & Zero-Trust Edge (15 members):** Mainstream 08-19 (75) → 08-20 (74) → 08-21 (74) → 08-24 (71) → **Fading 08-25 (66) → Fading 08-26 (68)** → **Mainstream 08-27 (77)**. CRWD's EP alerted on 08-27. ⚖ **THE MECHANISM:** `in_active_theme` counts only `Accelerating`/`Mainstream` (`ep_detector` ~:2897), so on the two Fading days the name scores ZERO theme credit — and the score is computed from the theme's state BEFORE the EP, while the EP is often what re-rates the group. The score therefore withholds the bonus at precisely the moment the theme is turning. Evidence only. Raw: `docs/analysis/414_detection_anchored_orb_2026-09-06.txt`. >> 🗄 **SHELVED 2026-09-06 by the operator (*"got it, let's shelve this"*). NOT closed, NOT deleted — do not pick this up again without him.** ▶ **WHAT IS SETTLED and needs no re-derivation: (1) widening the ORB submission window 09:45→10:00 costs **−11.18R over 26 trades in 90 days** to buy one +4.25R winner (TSAT) — measured on a VALID test with the range anchored to detection; (2) the earlier "structurally unfillable / the window filters losers" reading was an ARTIFACT of pricing a 09:50 detection off the 09:30 bar, and is withdrawn; (3) the cohort is NOT tail-less — 1 of 26 ≥3R (3.8%) against 0 of 66 on the main admitted book.** ▶ **WHAT WOULD REOPEN IT, so the shelf is not a black hole: a DIFFERENT ENTRY GEOMETRY for late detections — a 5-minute range from detection, an ADR-based stop, a pullback entry or a capped chase. AKTS is the standing exhibit (+47% stock peak, −3.50R trade, stop set off one volatile minute), and that is a geometry failure, not a verdict on the names.** ▶ **KEPT AND STILL LIVE regardless of the shelf: `RuleSet.submit_window_end` (default `time(9,45)` = the live rule, pinned by `tests/test_414_submit_window_variant.py`) and `scripts/probes/_414_detection_anchored_orb.py` — so whoever reopens this runs one command instead of rebuilding the counterfactual.** >> ✅ **2026-09-08 — THE VERIFY IS NO LONGER UNOBSERVABLE.** The D1 same-day exclusion happens in the SQL (`alert_date <> $1`), so excluded rows never reached `examined` and the audit row said nothing about them — which is why this sat since August as *"cannot be manufactured"*. **It was being met constantly: 15 of the last 17 fills landed before 09:35.** The 09:35 pass now reports `same-day excluded N (tickers)` (`live_tracker._stop_refresh` + `db.get_same_day_excluded_stop_candidates`), a real zero when none, and nothing on the post-close pass which has nothing to exclude. Telemetry only — which rows get refreshed is unchanged. Shipped in `2be7e45e` (whose message documents only the regime fix — see CHANGELOG 2026-09-08). **VERIFY: the next 09:35 pass with a same-day fill open reports a non-zero count.** >> 🔎 **CHECKED IN PROD 2026-09-11 and the verify is NOT met — and the reason is worth writing down, because `same-day excluded 0` looks like a pass.** ▶ The morning refresh has logged `same-day excluded 0 (none)` on 09-09, 09-10 and 09-11. **That zero is not evidence:** it is produced identically by *no same-day fill happened* and by *the exclusion is broken*. ▶ **The one day that WOULD have exercised it predates the counter.** On **2026-09-08 PHVS filled 09:31 and SEI filled 09:35** — the same minute as the refresh — and that day's row reads `Morning: examined 2, placed 0, already-covered 2, skipped 0` with **no same-day clause at all**, so the telemetry shipped after the only qualifying morning. ▶ **The OTHER half has never fired either:** every morning since reads `placed 0, already-covered N`, so no Day-2+ stop has actually been MOVED at 9:35 (the one `placed 1` is a 09-08 POST-CLOSE run, a different job). ▶ **SHARPENED VERIFY, both halves in one morning:** a morning refresh on a day with a same-day fill must log **`same-day excluded ≥ 1`** AND **`placed ≥ 1`** for a Day-2+ position. WOULD-FAIL-IF: a same-day fill exists that morning and the excluded count still reads 0. ⚠ Event-gated on a fill, which cannot be manufactured. [swept:2026-09-11:67911155]



- #597 | 2026-09-30 | deployed | 🐛 **If a position vanishes from the broker mid-flight, we close the row with no exit leg and book the wrong P&L.** Found 2026-08-25 by the deployed-broker review. `sync_positions`' "position gone from Alpaca" branch (`order_manager.py:5805-5816`) sets `remaining_shares=0` and closes the trade **without recording an exit leg**. It can race a #591 stays-open row: the resting exit fills at the broker, our websocket finaliser has not caught up, `sync_positions` polls in that gap, and the row closes with the fill unrecorded. **The booked P&L is then wrong and no exit leg explains it.** ✅ It is now DETECTED rather than silent — the #588 L1 invariant `exit_share_sum_mismatch` fires on exactly this shape, and its docstring's claim that the state "can no longer exist" is true only for the paths #591 touched, not for this one. ▶ **DoD: make that branch record the exit it implies (or refuse to close and let the finaliser do it), so the invariant stops being the only thing standing between a race and a wrong number.** ⚠ Detection is not correction: by the time the L1 fires, the trade's P&L is already wrong and feeds `mi_sell_discipline_records`, which every exit-rule replay reads. ⚖ **Money path — `broker/` runs on apollo-execution, needs `deploy.sh execution` as a second step.** Recording bug, not a discipline change, but a change to when a row closes is his call. Same file and class as **#596** (a partly-filled leg cancelled with no replacement) — worth doing together. [Operational safety / hardening] ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-29 (Fable card, verified inline before acceptance).** New `order_manager._resolve_position_gone`, a HYBRID of the DoD's two options because each alone re-creates a known failure: blind-close books $0 on a real loss (the bug), and refuse-only leaves a phantom open row whenever the WS event is genuinely LOST. Under the #151 per-trade advisory try-lock, re-reading the row with the `account_mode` filter: **(1) GRACE** — stop confirmed filled <10 min ago → defer, the finaliser owns it; **(2) RECORD** — older fill → book the exit from BROKER TRUTH ONLY (`filled_avg_price`/`filled_qty`/`filled_at`), exits + `total_pnl` + `remaining_shares` in ONE statement (the MNDY 2026-05-11 invariant), idempotent on `exits[].order_id`; **(3) REFUSE** — no confirmable fill → **row stays OPEN and LOUD** (audit + Telegram + a discrepancy line every sweep). ⚠ **NEVER SYNTHESISES A PRICE** — a wrong-but-confident P&L is the exact failure being fixed. ⚖ **CLOSE-TIMING CHANGED and it is declared in the code comment** (was: unconditional same-sweep close; now: same-sweep only on a confirmed fill) — recording only, no order placed/cancelled/altered, revert = delete the helper and restore the blind UPDATE. 9 tests, mutation-verified by the card (pre-fix 9/9 red; exit-leg removed, idempotency no-op'd, grace zeroed, total_pnl decoupled — all red) **and independently by me: mutating it to accept an unconfirmed fill turned 2 tests red.** ⚠ **A FOURTH GATE — `audit_column_writes` (trade-state ownership, the BW/KLAR/CRMD class) — REJECTED THE FIRST CUT with exit 6, and it was right:** the helper wrote `exits` / `remaining_shares` / `total_pnl` / `closed_at` / `status` itself, which would have made a SIXTH writer of trade-state columns. The registry's own history records that T1.3 on 2026-05-18 REMOVED a duplicate writer for exactly this reason (*"close-path delegated to finalize_stop_fill which is the canonical writer"*), so the gate's option (a) — register the new writer — would have undone that consolidation. **Took option (b):** case 2's commit now delegates to `_finalize_stop_fill_locked`, and the card's own idempotency guard + Telegram were DELETED as redundant rather than left as a second copy (the canonical writer owns both, and also nulls `stop_order_id`). ✅ **The deadlock risk I flagged was checked, not assumed:** the lock is NOT re-entrant cross-connection, so the public wrapper would self-deadlock — it calls the `_locked` variant while holding the try-lock. **I verified independently that the `_locked` variant never acquires** (zero lock calls in its body). Gate now exit 0. Suite 6534. ▶ **VERIFY-LIVE = the next `sync_positions` sweep logs no `sync_position_gone_unresolved` for a healthy book, and any real occurrence Telegrams instead of silently closing.** Needs `deploy.sh execution` (broker/ runs there). [b1] >> ⏳ **EVENT-GATED, and I am NOT ticking it on an absence.** Checked prod 09-01: ZERO `sync_position_gone_unresolved` rows ever. That is CONSISTENT with the fix and PROVES nothing — the guarded path only runs when a position actually vanishes from the broker mid-flight, which has not happened. [[a-rule-is-not-live-until-it-has-fired-once]] **CLEARING CONDITION: the first real vanish event Telegrams instead of silently closing the row.** Until then it is unverifiable by construction, and re-dating monthly on a false 'looks fine' would be the fake-verify this board keeps paying for.

- #600 | 2026-09-30 | deployed | ⚠ **The repair's place branch can re-arm a stop at a price the broker had already moved past — now reachable every 5 minutes instead of once a day.** Found 2026-08-26. `order_manager.py:5506-5510` calls `alpaca.place_stop_order(..., float(db_stop_price), ...)` directly, bypassing `update_stop`'s raise-only floor against broker truth (added 2026-08-10). The stale-price window has exactly three sources, all in `execute_partial_exit`'s breakeven-replace (`:3670`, `:3675-3730`, `:3817-3843`), which keep the stop pointer while withholding the price because the replace's outcome was unconfirmed. **Worst case is bounded at ~1R** (`breakeven_price − original_stop`, ≈$20-50 at today's sizing). Real-money analog for the same pathology via a different trigger: **FIGS 2026-08-07, $13.74 on 41 shares** (`docs/setups/exit_discipline.md`, 08-08 entry). ▶ **DoD: route the place branch through the same raise-only floor, or state why it must not be.** ⚠ Pre-existing mechanism — this week's retry did not create it, it raised the exposure from once at 16:05 to every 5 minutes for the life of a failed-repair episode. ⚖ Money path, `broker/` → `deploy.sh execution`. [Operational safety / hardening] >> ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-03 (Fable card) — BUT NOT THE WAY THE DoD'S FIRST HALF ASSUMED, and the card's reasoning for that is right. Suite 7255. NOT DEPLOYED — money path, needs his sign-off.** ▶ **It did NOT route through `update_stop`'s floor, and here is why that would have been wrong: that floor REFUSES against a LIVE broker stop, and a re-protect places precisely BECAUSE there is no live stop** — so it would have applied no floor at all. It also sizes from DB `remaining_shares` where the coverage invariant deliberately sizes from BROKER qty (the 109-vs-28 incident), and it cancels the pointer first. **That is the DoD's second half — *state why it must not be* — answered properly.** ▶ **Instead: one pure `_floor_reprotect_price(base, broker_order)` — the placed price is the DB price RAISED to the broker stop's own price, never lowered.** ✅ **THE SAFETY PROPERTY I WAS MOST WORRIED ABOUT IS VERIFIED BY RUNNING IT, not by reading the report: with NO broker truth it PLACES AT THE DB PRICE AND NEVER REFUSES.** Checked all six paths directly — no broker order · empty dict · no `stop_price` · broker stop lower · `rejected` status (never live) all return the base price unchanged; only a genuinely higher broker stop raises it (10.00 → 10.75). **A floor that could refuse would turn a bounded ~1R defect into an unprotected position, which is far worse than the bug.** ▶ **FIVE SITES, not one** — the card checked the class, which is the pattern that has bitten three times this week: the coverage place branch (the ticket), sync orphan remediation (**this is the ticket's *"once at 16:05"***), both WS cancel→re-place paths in `trade_stream`, and `update_stop`'s terminal-old-stop carve-out. **And it found a FOURTH stale source the ticket did not list: the market-mode fold-in (3:45 ladder, `/partialnow`), outside the breakeven-replace entirely.** ⚖ **TWO FORKS FOR HIM, surfaced not decided:** **(1)** when the market is already through the last broker level, the floored placement now BREACHES → converge + one alert (identical to an accurate-DB breach, signed 06-23) rather than accidentally re-arming at the original stop — card recommends keeping that; the alternative is a new two-tier stop rule. **(2) REACH: `_handle_cancel_or_reject` §2 NULLs the pointer on the WS cancel/expiry (signed T1.5a) BEFORE any re-protect can read it**, so on the common intraday path the retry still lands at the DB price — that handler holds the cancelled stop's price in hand and preserving it is his call. ⚠ **Deploy needs BOTH `market-agent` AND `execution`, and his sign-off first.** ⚠ Historical context measured today: `stop_coverage_repaired` has fired **twice ever, both 2026-06-23, both PAPER**, and both were QUANTITY repairs — this is a latent defect, never a live-money loss. ⚖ **BOTH FORKS RULED BY HIM 2026-09-03 — neither is open any more.** **(1) Breach-and-converge: KEEP, closed** — it is what he signed on 06-23 for any breach; do not re-open. **(2) Cancel handler: APPROVED, BUILD FRIDAY 2026-09-04** (*"go with the rec, build fork 2 friday"*) — `_handle_cancel_or_reject` §2 discards the dead stop's price before any re-protect can read it, so **without this Friday build the #600 floor is DARK on the common intraday path** and only covers the rarer restore/sync/restart routes. Scope: preserve the level at the point the pointer is nulled; the T1.5a assume-naked fail-safe itself does NOT change. **SONNET-class — Fable capacity is gone until Friday evening.** ETA moved 09-05 → **09-04** so Friday's `--today` surfaces it; deploy #600 + fork 2 together (`market-agent` AND `execution`, in a window, his sign-off), then flip to `deployed` with a verify-date. Full reasoning + the ruling text: `docs/setups/exit_discipline.md` 2026-09-03 entry. >> ✅ **FORK 2 BUILT 2026-09-04 (his approval 09-03), suite 7357, NOT deployed.** The WS cancel handler now preserves the dying stop's own price and status immediately before it nulls the pointer; **the T1.5a assume-naked null is byte-for-byte unchanged** and an ordering test pins that. **I re-ran all five safety paths myself rather than trusting the card: nothing preserved · the preserved read raising · a preserved price BELOW base · above base · a preserved `rejected` order. Only a genuinely higher preserved price raises the placement; every other path places at base. It cannot refuse and cannot lower.** Staleness = a strictly-higher-only ratchet per trade in one atomic UPDATE; I checked the premise it rests on — *"widen"* in this codebase widens QUANTITY, not a price, so nothing legitimately lowers a live stop and the raise-only assumption holds. 4 new `mi_live_trades` columns via the house `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` pattern (312 existing precedents). ▶ ⚖ **POSSIBLE FORK 3 — HIS CALL, FOUND NOT BUILT: the same discard-the-price shape exists at FOUR more sites inside `execute_partial_exit`** (`partial_naked`, `partial_verify_stop_dead`, both `breakeven_replace_failed` nulls) **plus a fifth, weaker one in `update_stop`** (`stop_update_failed`). They are lighter: the stop dying at the broker almost always also fires the WS event, which now preserves the price first, so these only matter when the internal REST poll wins that race. **Today's infrastructure generalizes to all of them with ONE call each** — it is wiring, not design. Do not build it without asking him. >> ✅ **DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED LOADED 2026-09-04 — but it CANNOT close on a calendar, and I am not going to pretend otherwise.** Proven inside the running containers, not inferred: `_floor_reprotect_price` in **apollo-execution** raises 10.00 → 10.75 on a dead broker stop; `_preserve_dead_stop_price` (fork 2) is present; the floor is loaded in **apollo-market** too; all **four `dead_stop_*` columns exist in prod**. ▶ ⏳ **What is NOT verified is the floor ACTING, and it has had no occasion to: `stop_reprotect_floor_applied` = 0, and there were ZERO re-protect events of any kind today.** The historical base rate is **two firings in the system's life** (both 2026-06-23, both paper) — **so a date-based ETA on this task would be a lie that rebumps forever.** ▶ **Now EVENT-GATED instead: `data_gated_reviews.yaml::stop_reprotect_floor_first_fire_600` surfaces it the moment the floor writes its first row.** Close it then, after reading the row: the placed price must be the HIGHER of the two and the position must have ended protected. **VERIFY-LIVE = the first `stop_reprotect_floor_applied` row in `mi_audit_log`; read it and confirm the placed price is the HIGHER of the DB price and the dead stop's broker price, and that the position ended up protected.** >> ⏳ **2026-09-09 — RE-DATED, NOT SLIPPED: the gating event has never happened.** `stop_reprotect_floor_applied` has **zero rows in `mi_audit_log`, ever** — checked on prod today. The verify names that row as its evidence, so there is nothing to read and nothing I can do to make it fire; forcing one would mean manufacturing a broken-stop condition on live money. ▶ **It is already carried mechanically** by `stop_reprotect_floor_first_fire_600` in data_gated_reviews.yaml (threshold 1, earliest 2026-09-08), so the review resurfaces itself on the first real fire whatever this date says. 09-30 is an honest horizon, not a prediction. [b1] >> 🔎 **FLAGGED BY THE NEW ABSENCE-ONLY GATE 2026-09-10, and the flag was fair.** Its DoD — *route the place branch through the same raise-only floor, or state why it must not be* — is a design INSTRUCTION, not something anyone can read off prod, so nothing could ever confirm or refute it. ▶ **WOULD-FAIL-IF: a `stop_reprotect_floor_applied` row appears whose new stop is BELOW the prior stop — i.e. the floor let a stop be lowered, which is the exact harm the raise-only rule exists to prevent.** That is the reading that would mean this shipped broken. ▶ The positive observable stays what the line already says: the first such row, on any real repair. `stop_reprotect_floor_applied` has zero rows ever, so this is genuinely event-gated and `stop_reprotect_floor_first_fire_600` resurfaces it whatever this date says — but *event-gated* and *unverifiable* are different things, and it was written as the second.

## Apollo Themes (portfolio-app2)
- #580 | 2026-09-30 | pending | **Theme strength must be reported WITH breadth, and the one-sided trim must be measured before it is trusted — the operator found `/theme` ranking biotech #2 while the dashboard ranked it #3 (2026-08-20) and ruled that neither surface is the answer: *"you named flaws in both… the solution is not one or the other, but a 3rd better option."* He is right.** 🔎 **THE TWO SURFACES DIVERGE FOR THREE REASONS, all confirmed in code:** (a) `/theme` (`agent.py:4822` → `briefing.py:549` `_compute_scored_themes`) RECOMPUTES from current constituent RS, while `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` reads the STORED `mi_themes.score` written by the previous nightly run; (b) `/theme` excludes Fading, the dashboard applies no Fading/Retired filter; (c) the dashboard ranks inside a WEEKLY bucket (`compute_weekly_rank`, `drop_duplicates(['week_start','name'], keep='last')`), so a Monday row can rank against a Thursday row. Verified against prod the same day: both produce an identical top-5 once 'latest row per name' is applied, so the Retired gap (#214) was NOT the active cause — freshness + Fading + bucketing are. 🚩 **THE DEFECT UNDERNEATH BOTH: a theme is reduced to ONE averaged number, and that average is computed by a ONE-SIDED trim.** `constants.trimmed_mean` sorts ascending and drops the lowest N (`sorted_vals[drop:]`) — the top is NEVER trimmed. Its docstring claims outlier resistance, but a real trimmed mean cuts both tails; this one only ever deletes losers, so every theme scores structurally high, always in the same direction. **At our actual basket sizes it is not 20%:** the live top themes hold 3, 4, 5 and 3 stocks (prod, 2026-08-19), so a 3-stock theme drops 1 of 3 — 33% — and the 'score' is a two-stock average. A theme carried by two names and a theme where eight of ten participate are indistinguishable. 📉 **BREADTH IS ALREADY COMPUTED AND ALREADY LOAD-BEARING — it is simply never surfaced.** `mi_themes.pct_above_20sma` is written by `theme_engine.py:3179/3338` and already FORCES a theme to Fading below `_BREADTH_DECAY_THRESHOLD` (`theme_engine.py:3285`). ⚠ **But it is NULL on 29 of 120 rows (2026-08-19) — including all four of the top-scoring themes.** The newest, hottest themes are exactly the ones with no breadth number, which is the population where it matters most; root-cause that before surfacing it, or the new column renders blank precisely when he looks. ✅ **BUILD (three pieces, in this order):** (1) **Surface breadth next to strength** on `/theme` and on the dashboard — small, the number already exists and is stored; (2) **fix the NULL-on-new-theme gap** so the top themes carry a breadth figure (`theme_engine.py:6233` sets None on the Retired path; find the new-theme path that does the same); (3) **MEASURE the trim before changing it** — do NOT edit it blind: `trimmed_mean` has SEVEN call sites across `briefing.py` (4), `theme_engine.py` (2 — including the engine's own `momentum`, which drives stage decisions) and `theme_ecosystems.py` (2), so a change moves engine stage transitions and ecosystem scores, not just a display. Produce a $0 read-only before/after on stored cohorts: score with trim vs plain mean vs both-tails trim, and report how much rank order actually moves. 🏗 **ONE COMPUTATION, TWO LABELLED SURFACES.** `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` is a standalone streamlit+psycopg2 process with NO imports from `agents/`, so 'shared' cannot mean a shared import — it means the engine WRITES the canonical number and both surfaces READ it, with `/theme` labelled 'where strength is now' and the dashboard labelled 'what the engine acted on'. Today they are two unlabelled rankings, which is why a disagreement reads as a bug. 🧭 **NORTH STAR:** subtle RS → early theme → buy before mainstream (memory `theme-north-star-early-rs-before-mainstream`). Breadth is what separates a real early theme from two stocks with a story — the exact discrimination the north star needs. ⛔ **NOT before the weekend EP work** (P10: EP profitability before new setups). **DoD:** `/theme` and the dashboard both show strength + breadth from the same stored number; no NULL breadth on any non-Retired theme; a written before/after showing what the trim was doing to rank order, with a ruling on whether it stays. ➕ **A THIRD DISAGREEING COUNT, found 2026-08-21 — same defect, new surface.** The L2 anomaly alert reported `theme_count_active = 159` against a 30d median of 122, while `mi_themes` holds **113 non-Retired rows for that date**. The alert counts DISTINCT NAMES seen in the last 7 days (`get_active_themes(stale_after_days=7)`); the table counts today's rows. Neither is wrong; they answer different questions and **both are labelled 'active themes'**, so the alert fires on a definitional gap rather than a real jump. ⚠ The underlying row count IS drifting up on its own (100 → 107 → 120 → 127 → 128 over five days) — that is worth a look, but it is not what the alert measured. ▶ Fold this surface into the same fix: one computation, each surface labelled with the question it answers. [b2] [ok:2026-08-28→2026-09-30 — OPERATOR-DIRECTED PRIORITY RESET 2026-08-22 ("reset the board based on priority"): dates now express TIER, not accumulated drift. T4.] >> 🎯 **DoD (written 2026-09-10): ONE ranking, computed one way, reported WITH breadth — `/theme` and the dashboard return the same ordered list for the same date, and each entry carries its member count alongside its score.** WOULD-FAIL-IF: the two surfaces still disagree on any date, or a theme ranks highly on a score whose breadth the reader cannot see — which is the condition that produced the biotech #2-vs-#3 split. ⚖ His ruling stands: neither existing surface is the answer, so matching one to the other is NOT done.

He wants BOTH**, so Rank Flow is a surface to repair rather than retire — do not delete it in #561's name. ▶ **FOUR DEFECTS, three visible in his screenshot and one found in the code:** **(1) THE WORST ONE, from `theme_flow.py`'s own docstring: *"A cohort with no usable rank that week (never seen, a data gap, or genuinely fell out of the engine's top ranks) reads as 'Outside top 30' too."* So that band merges FOUR different facts — rank 31, rank 140, never-seen, and we-don't-know — and every ribbon into or out of it is therefore uninterpretable.** This is the SAME class as the `outside` mislabel fixed in Weekly Movers tonight (`5d119e0`), where 12 of 19 *entrants* turned out to have a known prior rank; here it is worse, because a data GAP is folded in with a weak rank. **(2)** Band labels render ON TOP of the ribbons and are half-unreadable — *Top 5*, *6-15*, *16-30* are all obscured in his screenshot. **(3)** Every ribbon is the same pale green, so nothing signals which move matters. **(4)** Most of the ink is the BORING case — thick horizontal ribbons are cohorts staying in their band, while the moves are hairlines; 4 bands × 11 weeks is too many ribbons to trace one cohort through. ▶ **(2)-(4) are quoted from #561's original diagnosis and were never fixed — #561 replaced the view instead of repairing it, and he has now asked for the repair.** 🎯 **DoD: he can read it on his phone and say what it tells him.** Concretely: the *outside* band separates *below the cut with a known rank* from *no rank at all*; labels legible; the ink weighted toward moves rather than stayers. WOULD-FAIL-IF: a ribbon still merges a data gap with a weak rank — that is the defect, not the styling. ⚠ **Readability is HIS call, as with #561** — build it and stop. ⚖ Reporting surface only. [Apollo Themes (portfolio-app2)] >> ✅ **BUILT AND PUSHED 2026-09-11 (`portfolio-app2` `b0820d5`) — Streamlit Cloud redeploys on push, so it is LIVE.** ▶ **Defect (1), the one the DoD names, is fixed completely:** the band is now two rows — `31+` (a known `week_rank` past the cut) and `No rank` (absent that week, or a null `rs_avg` gap) — reusing the distinction `theme_movers.py` already made. 🔢 **Measured on the real snapshot at the page's 10-week default: of 1230 cohort-weeks in the old merged band, 190 (15%) carried a known rank past the cut.** (Different denominator from Weekly Movers' 12-of-19, which counted weekly board ENTRANTS rather than cohort-week cells — said here so the two numbers are not read as contradicting.) ▶ (2) labels moved to a reserved left gutter, (3) ribbons coloured by direction reusing `theme_grid`'s own up/down/flat vocabulary, (4) held-band ribbons dropped to 0.12 opacity. ⚠ **AND THE CARD'S COLOUR RULE CARRIED THE SAME DEFECT ONE LAYER DOWN:** it painted `31+ → No rank` RED as a demotion because No-rank sits at the bottom of `_BAND_RANK`, and pinned that with a test. But the band means *absent OR a data gap*, so red asserts weakness about a cohort that may simply have no data — the task's own defect moved into the colour channel. Any ribbon touching `No rank` is now neutral at 0.45 opacity; the card's test is reversed with the reasoning inside it. 20 tests on this view, 111 across the repo. ▶ **VERIFY 2026-09-14 = HIS EYES.** The rendered page was NOT opened (SSO-gated); verified by AppTest against the committed snapshot and by rendering the Plotly figure to PNG in both themes. The DoD is *he can read it on his phone and say what it tells him*, and only he can close that. - #639 | 2026-09-12 | in_progress | 🟠 **TWO THIRDS OF THE THEME BOARD IS NEW EVERY WEEK, AND NOBODY OWNS THAT — measured 2026-09-10 when the operator asked whether churn was covered.** ▶ **THE NUMBER, from the canonical weekly grid the dashboard already computes: entrants to the top 30, week by week — 19 (63%) · 13 · 12 · 10 · 15 · 17 · 14 · 14. Between a third and two thirds of a 30-slot board is a name that was not there seven days earlier, every week for two months.** ▶ **Why it surfaced: #561's new Weekly Movers view leads with *new to the top 30*, and at 19 of 30 that block is the BORING case — the same defect Rank Flow had (*almost all the ink is cohorts staying put*), arriving in a new shape.** A theme reaching the top 30 is not a signal if most of the board does it weekly. ▶ ⛔ **NOT COVERED BY #553/#555, and #553 says so itself: *"Same family as apollo-side theme churn (#476 name/member instability, #471 re-granularization) but this is the DASHBOARD's own matcher, a separate code path — do not conflate."*** Those fixed FALSE MERGES — things wrongly joined. Churn is the opposite: identity not surviving from one week to the next. ▶ **AND THE OWNER IS GONE: #476 was closed 2026-07-17 for unrelated reasons** (per-family cap verified, pool fix verified-safe, elite coverage reframed), **not because instability was solved** — and #471, the other sibling it names, I closed today. So nothing on the board owns this. 🎯 **DoD: separate REAL rotation from IDENTITY INSTABILITY, and say which dominates.** For each week's entrants, is the cohort genuinely new (its tickers were not together on the board under any name) or is it an existing cohort under a changed name/membership? The canonical grid carries `canonical_id`, so this is a $0 read of data already on disk. WOULD-FAIL-IF: the answer is quoted as a single churn percentage without that split — a renamed cohort and a genuinely new theme are different findings with different fixes. ⚖ Measurement only; no engine, grade or ranking change without CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off. [[themes-not-judged-on-returns-yet]] >> 🔑 **THE OPERATOR'S OWN OBSERVATION CRACKED IT, 2026-09-10: *"I find it odd there's so much churn in the dash but I don't notice it in daily summaries which also shows the themes, but one is weekly vs daily."*** He is right that they disagree, and the cause is NOT mainly cadence. ▶ **THE DAILY BRIEF CANNOT SHOW CHURN BY CONSTRUCTION.** `brief_composer.compute_theme_movers` keys on the theme **NAME** and does `prior = theme_prior.get(name)` / **`if not prior: continue`** — so a theme with no prior-day row under the SAME NAME is silently SKIPPED, never reported. **A brand-new theme and a RENAMED one are exactly the rows that have no prior under that name, so the one surface he reads daily is structurally blind to the very thing the dashboard is screaming about.** ▶ **THREE DIFFERENCES, not one, and the identity one is the big one:** (a) **IDENTITY** — the brief keys on `name`, the dashboard on `canonical_id` from its own matcher, which tracks a cohort THROUGH a rename; (b) **METRIC** — the brief measures a SCORE delta (`comp` vs prior `rs_avg`, itemised only past `THEME_ITEMIZE_DELTA`), the dashboard measures RANK movement on a 30-slot board, and a theme can enter the board because others FELL; (c) **CADENCE** — day vs week, the smallest of the three. ▶ **This makes the #639 split sharper, not just possible: the brief's skipped-for-no-prior rows ARE the churn population.** Counting them per day is a direct measure and needs no new pipeline. ⚠ **And it is a finding about the brief in its own right — a daily surface that drops new and renamed themes is under-reporting, not quiet.** [[prove-two-numbers-measure-the-same-thing]] >> 🔬 **MEASURED 2026-09-10 — HE ASKED IF THE CHURN IS ARTIFICIAL (JUST RENAMES). IT IS NOT, AND IT IS ALSO NOT MOSTLY CHURN.** Of this week's **19 entrants to the top 30**: **12 EXISTED LAST WEEK UNDER THE SAME `canonical_id`, ranked below 30** · 11 share ≥0.50 ticker overlap with some theme last week · **only 7 are a genuinely new basket.** Last week held **143 themes for 30 board slots**. ▶ **So the dominant effect is RANK ROTATION THROUGH A HARD TOP-30 CUT of a 143-theme population — not naming, and not identity instability.** A theme moving 34 → 12 is invisible day to day and reads as an *entrant* only because the board truncates at 30. ▶ ⛔ **THIS CORRECTS WHAT I TOLD HIM EARLIER TODAY.** I said *"19 of 30 slots are new — a theme reaching the top 30 isn't a signal"* and implied instability. **12 of those 19 were on last week's list; I had compared against the BOARD only, not against all 143 themes.** The narrower population made ordinary rotation look like churn — the same population error that has bitten twice this week already. ▶ 🎯 **AND IT EXPOSES A MISLABEL IN THE VIEW I SHIPPED TONIGHT: *New to the top 30* is literally true but reads as *new theme*, and 12 of 19 are not new.** Their real prior rank IS in the grid, so they can render as climbers — *34 → 12 (+22)* — instead of *outside → 12*. `_OUTSIDE_BOUND` collapses everything past 30 to *outside* when the number is known. **His call whether to change it; he has already said the view is fine.** ▶ **What remains genuinely open for this task: the 7 new baskets a week — is that real rotation or the engine minting cohorts?** >> ✅ **THE MISLABEL IS FIXED AND LIVE 2026-09-10 (`portfolio-app2` `5d119e0`, Streamlit redeploys on push) — operator: *"add all context into task and fix it together then"*.** ▶ **`compute_weekly_movers` treated ANY cohort ranked worse than 30 last week as *entered from outside*. But 45 is a number we have.** Only a cohort with NO rank at all last week — absent, or present but unranked — is now a first appearance; anything with a known prior rank is a climber with a real delta. ▶ **EFFECT ON THIS WEEK'S VIEW: entrants 19 → 9, climbers 5 → 15, and the week's BIGGEST REAL MOVE surfaces for the first time — *Pure-Play NAND/DRAM Memory Chip Makers 90 → 18 (+72)*, previously rendered *outside → 18* and sorted among entrants by where it landed rather than by how far it climbed.** ⚠ **That is precisely the failure #561 exists to fix, reproduced by the new view on its own first day** — the boring case taking the ink while the big move hid inside it. ▶ **The test that asserted the old behaviour was REWRITTEN with the measurement in its docstring, not deleted**, plus one pinning that a below-the-cut cohort lands in exactly one bucket. 91 passed. ▶ 📋 **CONTEXT NOW CONSOLIDATED ON THIS LINE — the chain, in order: he noticed the dashboard churning while his daily summaries did not → the daily brief keys on NAME and skips any theme with no prior-day row under that name, so it is structurally blind to new and renamed themes → the dashboard keys on `canonical_id` and sees them → measuring the split showed 12 of 19 were already on last week's list, 7 genuinely new → which exposed *outside* as a label the data did not support.** **What is STILL open and is what this task is now about: the ~7 genuinely new baskets a week — real rotation, or the engine minting cohorts?** >> ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-09-11, $0, `docs/analysis/639_board_churn_split_2026-09-11.md` (probe `portfolio-app2/_639_churn_split.py`).** ▶ **RENAMING IS NOT THE CAUSE, and it is settled: 1 RENAMED entrant in 11 post-launch weeks out of 152, at every overlap threshold tested (0.50 / 0.60 / 0.75).** #476 and #471 were the right suspects and they are cleared. Post-launch split: **NEW 64-76% · REAPPEARED 24-35% · RENAMED 0-1.** ⚠ **Two corrections the first run needed:** comparing only against LAST week scored 2026-06-22 as 24 entrants all-new (a canonical-id reset signature, not rotation) — every basket is now checked against every basket ever seen; and **06-22 is the LAUNCH, an era boundary**, so everything is cut post-launch rather than averaged across it. 🔑 **THE FINDING UNDERNEATH: 71% of the genuinely-new entrants carry ≤3 tickers (median 3) against 39% of the reappearing ones (median 4). The board churns because it keeps admitting tiny freshly-assembled baskets.** ⚠ **And the measurement is weakest exactly where the mass is** — a 2-ticker basket sharing one ticker with a prior 3-ticker basket scores 0.25 and reads NEW, so 63-70% is an UPPER BOUND on novelty, not a measurement. ⚖ **WHAT IT OPENS, AND IT IS HIS CALL: should a two- or three-ticker cohort hold a slot on a thirty-slot board?** Most of the churn is those slots turning over. That is a board-composition rule, not a matcher fix, and this produces evidence for it rather than proposing it. ▶ **The task stays open for his ruling on that one question** — the DoD's split is delivered.

- #505 | 2026-09-24 | pending | **PARENT-CHILD must hold on EVERY discovery path (operator 2026-07-27: "parent child relationship must work regardless on how themes are discovered, if not then it's broken").** TODAY IT DOES NOT: `parent_theme` is set on **1 of 94** live themes. ROOT CAUSE TRACED — parenting is a SIDE EFFECT of one narrow path (`theme_engine.py:~4638`): it runs ONLY when a NEWBORN theme would otherwise be STRIPPED for overlap and the ADR-0025 adjudicator returns PARENT_CHILD (the log says 'strip averted'). It never runs for existing themes, never retroactively, and **the `shadow_promoted` auto-promote path writes AFTER that step and owns its own rows (theme_engine.py:1926-27), so it NEVER reaches the adjudicator** — which is how Lane-2 (#167) output arrives. `theme_subtheme_routed` has fired **ONCE (2026-07-17)**, 11 days after the #471 arm flip. ⚠ **NOT the ecosystem layer — that is COMPLETE AND HEALTHY** (94/94 mapped, 20 e_codes, catch-all EMPTY); I wrongly called the hierarchy broken before checking both layers. Ecosystem = CLASSIFICATION (fixed buckets, unconditional); parent_theme = CONTAINMENT (one theme inside another). ⚖ **NOT filed on #471 deliberately — #471 IS the strip-aversion path this must be DECOUPLED FROM.** DoD: parenting runs regardless of discovery path incl. `shadow_promoted`; **every theme has a parent even if a CATCH-ALL** (operator); a backfill pass over the existing 94; and the relationship RENDERED on at least one operator surface (see #506 — today it is in 4 internal files and ZERO surfaces). ⚖ Theme structure feeds the judge + allocation ⇒ CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off before any live behavior flip. **WHY IT MATTERS: a flat list of 94 peers cannot express 'these four are one bet' — the same concentration question #452 hit from the other end; E-SAAS alone holds 13 side-by-side themes.** Refs #471 (the narrow path), #167 (inherits the gap, does not cause it), #452. [b5] >> ⏱ **PULLED TO TODAY 2026-07-27 (operator: "theme I want to work on today, just later") — the 08-04 full-capacity date is retired; this is a TODAY task.** >> 📊 **EVIDENCE CAPTURED 2026-07-27 — the sprawl is MEASURED, and the L2 detector flagged it unprompted (operator: capture this so we have evidence).** TWO L2 anomalies fired tonight and they are the SAME phenomenon: `theme_count_active` 108 vs 30d median 86 (z=3.14) and `cooldowns_per_day` 11 vs median 2 (z=9.0, 5.5×). **NOT caused by today's deploys — verified:** the live-theme count climbed **80 (07-21) → 89 → 90 → 94 (07-24) → 94 (07-27)**, i.e. the growth predates every theme change shipped today. **BIRTH RATE IS THE DRIVER: 7 · 4 · 10 · 7 · 4 · 5 new themes born on 07-20…07-27 — roughly SIX PER DAY, every day.** The cooldown spike is the downstream consequence, not a separate fault: more themes ⇒ more membership validation ⇒ more removals (11 removals spread thin across 7 different themes, max 2 any one). ⚠ **AND THE SYSTEM HAS PRODUCED 143 THEMES THAT LATER WENT QUIET FOR >7 DAYS** — born, briefly alive, aged out. ⚖ **THE REFRAME THIS FORCES: ~6 births/day against a 7-day absence expiry is a system that GENERATES faster than it CONSOLIDATES. Parenting (#505) and the health check (#506) treat the SYMPTOM; the BIRTH RATE is the cause.** The design question is therefore not 'how do we nest 94 themes' but **'should six new themes a day exist at all, or should most of those be JOINING something?'** — which is exactly the question the #167 Lane-2 registry answered for narratives the same day: asking 'join or new?' against carried state collapsed **23 near-duplicate proposals into 4 real narratives**, at ~7× lower token cost. That mechanism is the candidate answer here, not a coincidence. Measured against the operator's north star [[theme-north-star-early-rs-before-mainstream]] — subtle RS → early theme → matures → buy before mainstream — a 6/day birth rate with 143 aged-out corpses is noise the operator must wade through, not edge. **Do NOT drop the scoped work (operator 2026-07-27: "don't just drop what we scoped until we design and eval") — this evidence RE-ORDERS it: lane/birth-rate consolidation is the DESIGN step that comes first, and #505/#506 follow whatever it decides.** >> ✅ **MEASUREMENT CORRECTED 2026-07-27 evening — parenting is 5 of 94, NOT 1 of 94.** I sampled mid-afternoon, BEFORE the 17:05 nightly ran. Against the north star the theme is the product and the cluster is an intermediate. Data keeps flowing to the engine; only the surface goes, and "show clusters" still works by typing. The stock was +32.6% by 09:35 and +33.5% by 09:40. **ALPACA'S OWN WORDS, recovered:** `"reason": "[6098] Stop Price Already Triggered/Exceeds $ Threshold"`. **WHERE IT HID:** not on the order object (checked every key of the raw REST payload) — it is on the trade-updates EVENT beside the order, and the SDK's `TradeUpdate` model has NO `reason` field, so `raw_data=False` parses it away before our handler runs. Every rejection we have ever had arrived with a reason we threw out. ⚠ **Hours were spent inferring** — ruling out buying power ($17,355 vs a $910 order), account state, symbol restrictions, an in-the-money trigger, and a paper probe (`scripts/probes/_540_oto_rejection_probe.py`, 6 cases) that DID find a real rule (`stop_loss.stop_price must be <= base_price - 0.01`, base_price = the LIMIT price) which **does not explain INSM** (126.15 ≤ 130.05 passes). ⚠ **An external research pass proposed 'market price was below the stop-loss' — FALSIFIED by our own data**: last was $128.96, above the $126.15 stop. **SHIPPED:** `alpaca_client.fetch_broker_reject_reason()` — a targeted lookup against the trade-events history, called ONLY on reject/cancel/expire, never on the fill path; the reason lands in the audit detail as `broker_reason` AND in the operator Telegram. Deliberately NOT `raw_data=True`: that would rewrite every handler on the FILL path to work on dicts to recover a field needed only on a terminal failure. Never raises; a missing reason degrades silently. 7 tests. - #545 | 2026-09-23 | deployed | 🔬 **ENTRY/EXIT TACTICS PROGRAM — the EP cohort wins, our tactics do not (operator 2026-08-07, his framing and it is the sharpest statement of the problem so far).** *"EP stocks is a winning cohort overall (not high win rate, but major winners can be found here) however, entry/exit tactics is the big challenge… I want to run multiple variations of all the parameters including some more novel approaches like re-enter next day, which is similar to delayed EP, or even next few days when some delayed setup hits."* **WHY THIS IS A PROGRAM AND NOT A TASK:** we have tried pieces of this in isolation — same-day re-entry (R3, killed on 0-for-7), the 5-min ORB re-entry replay (`orb_5m_reentry_hybrid_replay`, never run), the giveback peak-lock (#306, built dark), the +2R partial (live 08-05), the stop-floor (tested and REFUTED 08-06) — each answered one axis on one cohort and none of them swept the space. The operator is asking for the sweep. **THE THESIS TO TEST, stated as he stated it:** a low-win-rate / fat-right-tail population is one you HARVEST, not one you filter harder — so the leverage is in WHEN you enter and HOW you hold, not in tightening what qualifies. **SCOPE (design first, then execute):** (a) inventory every entry/exit variant already tried, with what it showed and on which cohort — several are recorded across #306/#503/#508/#541 and nowhere collected; (b) the parameter space itself — entry timing (ORB 1m/5m · next-day open · N-day delayed setup · re-entry after stop-out), stop basis (ORB low · ADR multiple · structure), profit-take (fixed R · ADR · trail · none), and hold rules; (c) which cells the EXISTING replay harnesses can answer with data already captured vs which need new capture — `_306_intraday_partial_sim.py`, `_508_exit_rule_replay.py` and `_stop_floor_forward_replay.py` already do minute-bar replay with a frozen fill contract, so reuse them rather than minting a fourth; (d) a ranked read with the cost of each variant stated in R, not just the benefit. ⚠ **REGIME IS A FIRST-CLASS AXIS, not a footnote:** ~~all 14 closed live trades were taken in Correcting/Choppy/Crisis and ZERO in Bull (measured 08-06)~~ **← STALE, CORRECTED 2026-09-02: there are now 26 closed live trades and TEN are stamped Bull; the review ran (see the Phase 4 note at the end of this line) and the caveat is RE-WORDED, not retired**, so every conclusion we hold is a non-bull conclusion — see `exit_tune_bull_regime_read`. ⚠ **NOT a licence to flip anything:** this produces EVIDENCE and a ranked recommendation; every live change is CHANGE_PROCESS + #151 harness + operator sign-off (THE LINE). **DoD:** a design doc with the variant inventory, the parameter grid, the answerable-vs-needs-capture split, and a phased execution plan for the operator to sequence. ⚖ Operator-carried 08-07. Feeds/overlaps #306 (exit tune) and `exit_tune_cohort_review` (the n=20/40/60 accrual vehicle) — this is the SUPERSET they roll up into, not a duplicate; sequence it so their next runs execute inside this frame. >> 🔗 **WIRED TO ITS TRIGGERS 2026-08-07 — operator: *"we already have triggers to eval once we have more samples, are they linked? We should connect all the entry/exit analysis work."*** They were not; the reference was one-way and weak. Now both directions. **THE TWO ACCRUAL VEHICLES, which fire on their own and feed THIS task:** (1) `exit_tune_cohort_review` — recurring at n=20/40/60/80/100 closed live trades (today 14), carrying methods (a) per-trade forensic · (b) intraday partial sweep · (c0) regime segmentation · (c) character segmentation · (d) holding-period distribution, and now (e) stop geometry; (2) `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` — fires at 8 live closes in a BULL tape (today 0), owning the one cell nothing else can fill, since all 14 closed trades were non-bull. **THE DIVISION OF LABOUR:** the reviews are the SAMPLE CLOCK — they say WHEN there is enough data. #545 is the FRAME — it says WHICH variants are worth accruing toward, including the ones no trigger currently watches (next-day re-entry · N-day delayed setup · entry timing variants). Neither replaces the other, and both now say so in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` so a future run reports INTO this task rather than writing an orphan dated doc that #545 has to rediscover. ⚠ Deliberately NOT re-keyed onto a single trigger: a program that only wakes at n=20 cannot design its own grid, and the design work (the inventory + the parameter space) is answerable NOW from data already captured. >> 📌 **FIRST REAL INSTANCE OF THE DELAYED-ENTRY VARIANT — operator's own discretionary fill on TEAM, 2026-08-07, recorded while it is fresh and before the outcome is known.** He took the SAME stock Apollo was stopped out of, hours later, and is holding. **THE TWO ENTRIES SIDE BY SIDE, from prod + SIP bars:** Apollo — ORB entry $147.13 at 09:31, stop $143.21 (ORB low), **stopped out −$23.94** inside ~20 minutes. Operator — **$144.39 at ~11:50 ET, stop at the low of day.** ⚠ **The low of day was $141.51 at 13:55 ET — AFTER his entry** — so his stop moved with the session; it was the low-so-far at entry, not a fixed level. **His stated method (his words, and the imprecision is the point):** *"my entry is looking at the low forming, turn back up, I also look at some other technicals… no hard rule so hard to copy."* **WHAT THE TAPE SHOWS around that entry:** TEAM based $142.90-143.90 for ~45 min (15:30-16:00Z), then stair-stepped up — 16:05 c=143.91 · 16:10 c=144.29 · 16:15 c=144.67 · 16:20 c=145.16. He bought into the third rising bar of that turn, not the low itself. **Day's range: high $151.37 at the open, low $141.51 at 13:55, last $148.42 at 17:45Z** — i.e. the stock round-tripped and then recovered above his entry. ⚖ **WHY THIS MATTERS TO #545 AND NOT JUST AS AN ANECDOTE:** it is the concrete form of the *next-day / delayed re-entry* variant this program was filed to test, and the operator's framing is that the EP COHORT is fine while the TACTICS are not — this is him demonstrating the tactic manually on the exact name the system rejected. **The measurable question it poses:** for every Day-1 stop-out, does a re-entry keyed on *base-then-turn* (rather than on the ORB) beat doing nothing? That is answerable OFFLINE on the recorded stop-out set — no new capture needed, `mi_intraday_bars` + the minute-bar harnesses already hold it. ⚠ **The hard part, stated honestly: "looking at the low forming, turn back up" is not yet a rule.** Before it can be swept it needs a mechanical proxy (e.g. N consecutive higher closes / higher lows off a session low, with a time window), and the proxy is a modelling CHOICE that will not be faithful to what he actually does. Sweep several proxies rather than pick one and claim it represents him. ▶ OUTCOME OF THIS TRADE TO BE RECORDED WHEN IT CLOSES — one fill is not evidence, but it is the fixture. >> 📚 **620-CHART CAPTURED 2026-08-07 → `docs/methodology/620_chart.md`.** The operator named it as part of the TEAM entry above and asked whether we still had a record of it — **we did not, in memory or the repo; it had been shared in an earlier session and lost.** Now written down from the full source (Gil Morales, theowltrader.com 2023-05-25). **THE SPEC:** 5-min candles · 6-EMA (orange) + 20-EMA (blue) · MACD on the SAME 6/20 periods; the eSignal `(6,20,C,9)` trailing digit is an auto-fill the author explicitly does not tune. **THE RULE THAT MATTERS FOR A SWEEP: the MACD cross is the SIGNAL; the 6-over-20 EMA cross is CONFIRMATION and fires ~1 hour later, by which point price is 'well up and away from the original entry'.** Modelling the MA cross as the trigger would systematically enter late. Also: MACD **stretch** (fast pulling away from slow) = extension, and its FLATTENING is his take-profit; the 20-EMA is the guide rail (resistance on a bounce from below, entry trigger on a pullback from above). ⚠ **TWO THINGS THE SOURCE SAYS THAT CONSTRAIN HOW WE TEST IT.** (1) **Parameter sensitivity is measured by the author himself** — he compares (6,20) / (8,24) / (12,26) and reports *the lower the setting, the earlier the signal AND the greater the chance it is false.* So periods are an AXIS in the sweep, not a constant. (2) **It is subordinate to price.** In his NVDA example the short entry came from the $400 Century Mark on price alone and the MACD cross was only confirmation — *"it was not necessary to use the MACD and/or moving averages to generate short-sale entries"*. He calls it *"a tool, NOT a trading system"* twice. ⇒ **A 620-only proxy will underperform what the operator actually does, and that is expected, not a bug in the test.** It is the one SPECIFIABLE piece of his 'other technicals' — computable from `mi_intraday_bars` (1-min from 07-25, resampleable to 5-min) — so sweep it as one factor among several rather than as the rule. >> ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-07 — 'ZERO bull-tape trades' IS NO LONGER TRUE, and the nightly digest caught it before I did.** The regime axis on this line (and on `exit_tune_bull_regime_read`) was measured 08-06 as Correcting 7 / Choppy 6 / Crisis 1, **zero Bull**. I repeated that on 08-07 without re-running it. **Today's three entries — NET, FIGS, TEAM — were all taken in a Bull tape and all three stopped out (−1.0R combined).** So the live cohort is now 17 trades: Correcting 7 · Choppy 7 · Bull 3 · Crisis 1, and `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` stands at **3 of 8, not 0**. ⚠ The structural point SURVIVES — 14 of 17 are still non-bull, so conclusions drawn from the July cohort remain non-bull conclusions — but the ABSOLUTE claim does not, and any sweep must stop asserting the cell is empty. **The early bull read is not encouraging: 3 entries, 3 stops, same day-one pattern.** ⚠ PROCESS NOTE worth more than the number: the `sell_discipline` digest printed the correct split (`regime@entry: Correcting 7 · Choppy 4 · Bull 3`) on the same evening I was telling the operator it was zero. **A figure measured yesterday and quoted today is not a measurement.** Re-run regime/cohort counts at the moment of use — they move every trading day. [[verify-before-reporting-to-the-operator]] >> 💡 **OPERATOR VARIANT TO SWEEP, 2026-08-08 — a SECOND partial higher up:** *"another variation we can take (once we have runners) is if it makes sense to take another 1/3rd profit somewhere higher, like 10R or etc. to make sure we don't round trip a big winner."* ⚖ This is the right shape for his stated goal — the first partial stops the papercuts, a second one banks the outsized move before it round trips, and neither caps the runner the way an exit-all would. **Sweep it as an AXIS (second-partial level: 5R/8R/10R/15R × fraction), not a single candidate.** ⚠ He gated it himself — *"once we have runners"* — and the live cohort has never had one (17 trades, 0 winners, max hold 2 days), so this is **paper/replay-only until the live book produces a survivor.** Feeds the same replay harness as #548's work (`_508_exit_rule_replay.py` already models multi-leg partials). >> 📊 **5m RE-ENTRY REVIEW RUN 2026-08-09 (ripe 39d, the oldest on the board) — N DOES NOT SUPPORT A CONCLUSION, and one trade carries the whole result.** Ran over the FULL live stop-out set (17, not a sample). **Fired on 9 of 17 (53%)**; all 8 non-fires are genuine (price never returned to clear the post-stop 5-min range), none mechanical. Of the 9: **1 real hold (THC +12.43R — but on a 0.67% stop, a razor-thin R-unit), 2 partial-then-scratch, and 6 paid a SECOND FULL STOP** (CRCL/FIGS/HUT/QBTS/WKC/WULF, verified against official daily lows). **Net +7.76R — but excluding THC the other 8 net -4.67R.** Same failure mode the 1m version (0-for-7) was killed for; the one real difference is selectivity (53% vs firing on nearly every stop-out). ⚠ It fired on 2 of the 4 biggest forward movers and **paid a second stop on both** (HUT despite a +8.32R forward peak, QBTS despite +8.88R). ⚠ The review's own SQL has no account-mode filter and counted 38 (26 paper + 12 live) — which is how it registered ripe; the replayable live set is 17. - #313 | 2026-10-27 | pending | P6 Institution-grade ops (H1→H2): #256 split (done) + replay-everything CI (#302) + cost governance (per-role LLM spend vs P&L) [b3] [ok:#419 Phase-2 milestone re-date, operator-signed 7/5] >> ✅ **COST-GOVERNANCE INCREMENT SHIPPED 2026-08-02 — spend is now ATTRIBUTABLE PER LANE, and a lane that switches on ANNOUNCES ITSELF.** Operator, after the chart-vision shadow ran unnoticed: *"we can have hidden costs that provides no value that is running and no way for us to know about in time."* ⚠ **THE COST STACK WAS NOT THE FAILURE** — the #377 meter, the #378 board/alarm and the #379 per-caller anomaly + reduction detectors all worked; they were reading a bucket with TWO THINGS IN IT. `grade_holistic`'s `log_caller` had a **DEFAULT** of `ep_grade_judge`; the chart shadow took it, and **336 of the 379 calls in that bucket over 30 days ($12.90) were an experiment indistinguishable from live grading.** The dollars were always derivable — the ATTRIBUTION was not. `judge_divergence` had overridden the default correctly and the docstring explained why, so convention existed, was documented, and the next lane still didn't follow it. **(1) The default is GONE** — `log_caller` is REQUIRED; every lane names itself. The sweep found **8 unlabelled call sites, not 1** — every offline eval and replay was ALSO billing the live judge (`tape_judge_eval`, `judge_model_eval`, `judge_enrich_eval`, `judge_backfill_replay`, `selection_replay_268`, `judge_integration_probe`, `chart_axis_shadow`, `chart_axis_eval` are now distinct buckets). **(2) NEW-LANE DETECTOR** (`cost_board._new_lanes_from_rows` + the 17:52 ET watchdog): a caller with spend in the last 3d and ZERO across the trailing 30d Telegrams ONCE, ever (audit-log dedupe, the same idiom the 7/17 budget-alarm re-fire fix used). This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution.
